qt_power_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_power_body

AI-generated explanation

qt_power_body defines a program expression with argument d: if d is at most zero, it returns the integer 1; otherwise, it calls qt_power with d − 1, binds the result to n, and returns n + n. Under the intended integer arithmetic and recursive-call interpretation, this computes 2 to the power d for nonnegative integer d and returns 1 for negative d. The input does not supply the full argument-binding semantics or establish that qt_power uses this body, and the definition alone proves neither correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func qt_power(d: Int64): Int64 {
    if (d <= 0) {
        1
    } else {
        let n = qt_power(d - 1)
        n + n
    }
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_power_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list("d")
            if (qle(d, qi(0))) {
                qi(1)
            } else {
                let n = qrecur("qt_power", list(d - qi(1)))
                n + n
            }

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qargs
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qi
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qle
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qrecur
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_power_body := qargs ["d"]
  (If (qle (Var "d") (qi 0)) (qi 1)
    (Let (Some "n") (qrecur "qt_power" [BinOp MinusOp (Var "d") (qi 1)])
      (BinOp PlusOp (Var "n") (Var "n")))).

qt_power

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_power

AI-generated explanation

qt_power e constructs a call to a recursive function named "qt_power", passing e as its single argument in a structure. Its body binds that argument as d, returns 1 when d is at most 0, and otherwise calls itself with d − 1 and doubles the result. With the expected integer arithmetic and helper meanings, this computes 2^d for positive integers and 1 for nonpositive integers; those helper meanings and runtime semantics are not fully supplied, and this definition alone establishes neither correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
qt_power(e)
Rocq structure
definition qt_power:
    parameter(e, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_power", qt_power_body, list(e))

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qinvoke

- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** Qᵣ(S ∪ T) = Qᵣ(S) ∪ Qᵣ(T), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove constructor, insertion, membership, and range-query contracts, then derive operation-composition theorems connecting the concrete programs to the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Coordinates and query endpoints use `Int64`; prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits under the depth restriction. All proofs must close without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and build integration

- Keep example-specific files under `quadtree/`, organized into `spec`, `algorithm`, `proof`, `extracted`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `results`, with local configuration, Makefile, and README.
- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.
- Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.
- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.
- Verification includes `coqchk`, assumption auditing, and checks against admissions or axioms.
- Generate quadtree-specific offline HTML inside `quadtree/docs/`, with links confined to that documentation.
- Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, exposing compiler and SDK paths as runner options.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate full results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_seed_body defines a program expression using qargs for the names node, x, and y, then sequentially stores the integer 1 in field 0 of node, the value of x in field 1, and the value of y in field 2, before returning unit. Here qargs is qargs_from starting at 0, but qargs_from is not supplied, so its precise argument-handling behavior is uncertain. The definition provides no correctness or termination contract and does not establish the runtime conditions under which these field stores succeed.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func qt_seed(node: QuadNode, x: Int64, y: Int64): Unit {
    node.tag = 1
    node.px = x
    node.py = y
    ()
}
Rocq structure

        qargs:

                    tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(0), items(qi(1)))

                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(1), items(x))

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

AI-generated explanation

qt_seed n x y constructs an expression that invokes qt_seed_body through a recursive closure named "qt_seed", packaging n, x, and y as a structure of arguments. The body appears intended to bind these arguments as node, x, and y, then store qi 1, x, and y at node indices 0, 1, and 2, followed by qu. The definitions of qargs, qstores, qi, and qu are not supplied, so their exact behavior is uncertain; no correctness or termination guarantee is established.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
qt_seed(n, x, y)

definition qt_seed:
    parameter(n, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(x, type(_ (inferred)))

qt_insert_branch

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert_branch

AI-generated explanation

qt_insert_branch defines a program expression that loads mx, my and four child references ll, lr, ul and ur from fields 7–12 of node, selects a child by comparing x with mx and y with my, and calls qt_insert_node with that child, d − 1, x and y. The selection uses ll when both comparisons hold, ul when only x < mx holds, lr when only y < my holds, and ur otherwise. Execution depends on valid field loads and suitable runtime bindings, whose details are not supplied; this definition alone establishes neither insertion correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
let mx = node.f7
let my = node.f8
let ll = node.f9
let lr = node.f10
let ul = node.f11
let ur = node.f12
let child = if (if (x <= mx) {
    if (x == mx) { false } else { true }
} else {
    false
}) {
    if (if (y <= my) {
        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { ll } else { ul }
} else {
    if (if (y <= my) {
        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { lr } else { ur }
}
qt_insert_node(child, d - 1, x, y)
Rocq structure
definition qt_insert_branch:
    body:
        qloads:
            "node"
            list_with_tail_items:
                tuple_with_tail_items("mx", items(in_scope nat(7)))
                items:
                    tuple_with_tail_items("my", items(in_scope nat(8)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("ll", items(in_scope nat(9)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("lr", items(in_scope nat(10)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("ul", items(in_scope nat(11)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("ur", items(in_scope nat(12)))
            let child = qchoose(x, y, mx, my, ll, lr, ul, ur)
            qrecur("qt_insert_node", list_with_tail_items(child, items(d - qi(1), x, y)))

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qchoose
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qi
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qloads
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qrecur
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_insert_branch :=
  qloads "node" [("mx",7%nat);("my",8%nat);("ll",9%nat);("lr",10%nat);("ul",11%nat);("ur",12%nat)]
    (Let (Some "child") (qchoose (Var "x") (Var "y") (Var "mx") (Var "my")
                           (Var "ll") (Var "lr") (Var "ul") (Var "ur"))
      (qrecur "qt_insert_node" [Var "child";BinOp MinusOp (Var "d") (qi 1);Var "x";Var "y"])).

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

push eerything including fnal report

qt_split

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_split

AI-generated explanation

qt_split defines a program expression that reads a node’s bounds, computes an offset using qt_power(d − 1), and uses it to form four quadrant bounds passed to qt_empty. It selects a quadrant for (px, py), with equality at a dividing coordinate going right or up, and calls qt_seed on it. It then stores 2, 0, and 0 in node fields 0–2, records the dividing coordinates and four children, and recursively inserts (x, y) into the selected child at depth d − 1. The supplied input does not establish the helper bodies’ behavior, the meanings of those numeric tags, valid-depth or memory premises, correctness, or termination; no WP contract is supplied.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
let x0 = node.f3
let y0 = node.f4
let x1 = node.f5
let y1 = node.f6
let half = qt_power(d - 1)
let split_mx = x0 + half
let split_my = y0 + half
let split_ll = qt_empty(x0, y0, split_mx, split_my)
let split_lr = qt_empty(split_mx, y0, x1, split_my)
let split_ul = qt_empty(x0, split_my, split_mx, y1)
let split_ur = qt_empty(split_mx, split_my, x1, y1)
let seed = if (if (px <= split_mx) {
    if (px == split_mx) { false } else { true }
} else {
    false
}) {
    if (if (py <= split_my) {
        if (py == split_my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { split_ll } else { split_ul }
} else {
    if (if (py <= split_my) {
        if (py == split_my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { split_lr } else { split_ur }
}
qt_seed(seed, px, py)
node.f0 = 2
node.f1 = 0
node.f2 = 0
node.f7 = split_mx
node.f8 = split_my
node.f9 = split_ll
node.f10 = split_lr
node.f11 = split_ul
node.f12 = split_ur
let mx = node.f7
let my = node.f8
let ll = node.f9
let lr = node.f10
let ul = node.f11
let ur = node.f12
let child = if (if (x <= mx) {
    if (x == mx) { false } else { true }
} else {
    false
}) {
    if (if (y <= my) {
        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { ll } else { ul }
} else {
    if (if (y <= my) {
        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) { lr } else { ur }
}
qt_insert_node(child, d - 1, x, y)
Rocq structure
definition qt_split:
    body:
        qloads:
            "node"
            list_with_tail_items:
                tuple_with_tail_items("x0", items(in_scope nat(3)))
                items:
                    tuple_with_tail_items("y0", items(in_scope nat(4)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("x1", items(in_scope nat(5)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("y1", items(in_scope nat(6)))
            let half = qt_power(d - qi(1))
            let split_mx = (x0 + half)
            let split_my = (y0 + half)
            let split_ll = qt_empty(x0, y0, split_mx, split_my)
            let split_lr = qt_empty(split_mx, y0, x1, split_my)
            let split_ul = qt_empty(x0, split_my, split_mx, y1)
            let split_ur = qt_empty(split_mx, split_my, x1, y1)
            let seed = 
                qchoose(px, py, split_mx, split_my, split_ll, split_lr, split_ul, split_ur)
            qt_seed(seed, px, py)
            qstores:
                "node"
                list_with_tail_items:
                    tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(0), items(qi(2)))
                    items:
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(1), items(qi(0)))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(2), items(qi(0)))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(7), items(split_mx))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(8), items(split_my))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(9), items(split_ll))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(10), items(split_lr))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(11), items(split_ul))
                        tuple_with_tail_items(in_scope nat(12), items(split_ur))
                qt_insert_branch

Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_split :=
  qloads "node" [("x0",3%nat);("y0",4%nat);("x1",5%nat);("y1",6%nat)]
  (Let (Some "half") (qt_power (BinOp MinusOp (Var "d") (qi 1)))
  (Let (Some "split_mx") (BinOp PlusOp (Var "x0") (Var "half"))
  (Let (Some "split_my") (BinOp PlusOp (Var "y0") (Var "half"))
  (Let (Some "split_ll") (qt_empty (Var "x0") (Var "y0") (Var "split_mx") (Var "split_my"))
  (Let (Some "split_lr") (qt_empty (Var "split_mx") (Var "y0") (Var "x1") (Var "split_my"))
  (Let (Some "split_ul") (qt_empty (Var "x0") (Var "split_my") (Var "split_mx") (Var "y1"))
  (Let (Some "split_ur") (qt_empty (Var "split_mx") (Var "split_my") (Var "x1") (Var "y1"))
  (Let (Some "seed") (qchoose (Var "px") (Var "py") (Var "split_mx") (Var "split_my")
                         (Var "split_ll") (Var "split_lr") (Var "split_ul") (Var "split_ur"))
  (Seq (qt_seed (Var "seed") (Var "px") (Var "py"))
  (qstores "node" [(0%nat,qi 2);(1%nat,qi 0);(2%nat,qi 0);(7%nat,Var "split_mx");(8%nat,Var "split_my");
                   (9%nat,Var "split_ll");(10%nat,Var "split_lr");(11%nat,Var "split_ul");(12%nat,Var "split_ur")]
    qt_insert_branch)))))))))).

Prompt 3 · 2026-10-10T13:18:56+00:00
push eerything including fnal report

qt_insert_node_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert_node_body

AI-generated explanation

This defines a quadtree insertion body with arguments node, depth d, and coordinates x and y. It reads the node’s tag: tag 0 calls the seeding routine and then returns true; tag 1 compares the stored point with (x,y), returning false if they match, otherwise creating four children, seeding one with the old point, updating the node to tag 2, and continuing insertion. Every other tag selects a child and recursively inserts with depth d−1. The input supplies no validity premises or correctness contract, and does not establish termination or the precise behavior of omitted helpers.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func qt_insert_node(node: QuadNode, d: Int64, x: Int64, y: Int64): Bool {
    let tag = node.tag
    if (tag == 0) {
        qt_seed(node, x, y)
        true
    } else {
        if (tag == 1) {
            let px = node.px
            let py = node.py
            if ((x == px) && (y == py)) {
                false
            } else {
                let x0 = node.x0
                let y0 = node.y0
                let x1 = node.x1
                let y1 = node.y1
                let half = qt_power(d - 1)
                let split_mx = x0 + half
                let split_my = y0 + half
                let split_ll = qt_empty(x0, y0, split_mx, split_my)
                let split_lr = qt_empty(split_mx, y0, x1, split_my)
                let split_ul = qt_empty(x0, split_my, split_mx, y1)
                let split_ur = qt_empty(split_mx, split_my, x1, y1)
                let seed = if (if (px <= split_mx) {
                    if (px == split_mx) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) {
                    if (if (py <= split_my) {
                        if (py == split_my) { false } else { true }
                    } else {
                        false
                    }) { split_ll } else { split_ul }
                } else {
                    if (if (py <= split_my) {
                        if (py == split_my) { false } else { true }
                    } else {
                        false
                    }) { split_lr } else { split_ur }
                }
                qt_seed(seed, px, py)
                node.tag = 2
                node.px = 0
                node.py = 0
                node.mx = split_mx
                node.my = split_my
                node.ll = split_ll
                node.lr = split_lr
                node.ul = split_ul
                node.ur = split_ur
                let mx = node.mx
                let my = node.my
                let ll = node.ll
                let lr = node.lr
                let ul = node.ul
                let ur = node.ur
                let child = if (if (x <= mx) {
                    if (x == mx) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) {
                    if (if (y <= my) {
                        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                    } else {
                        false
                    }) { ll } else { ul }
                } else {
                    if (if (y <= my) {
                        if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                    } else {
                        false
                    }) { lr } else { ur }
                }
                qt_insert_node(child, d - 1, x, y)
            }
        } else {
            let mx = node.mx
            let my = node.my
            let ll = node.ll
            let lr = node.lr
            let ul = node.ul
            let ur = node.ur
            let child = if (if (x <= mx) {
                if (x == mx) { false } else { true }
            } else {
                false
            }) {
                if (if (y <= my) {
                    if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) { ll } else { ul }
            } else {
                if (if (y <= my) {
                    if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) { lr } else { ur }
            }
            qt_insert_node(child, d - 1, x, y)
        }
    }
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_insert_node_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list_with_tail_items("node", items("d", "x", "y"))
            let tag = CJR FieldLoad [requires runtime or layout metadata](node, 0)
            if (qeq(tag, qi(0))) {
                qt_seed(node, x, y)
                qb(true)
            } else {
                if (qeq(tag, qi(1))) {
                    qloads:
                        "node"
                        list_with_tail_items:
                            tuple_with_tail_items("px", items(in_scope nat(1)))
                            items(tuple_with_tail_items("py", items(in_scope nat(2))))
                        if (qpoint_eq(x, y, px, py)) {
                            qb(false)
                        } else {
                            qt_split
                        }
                } else {
                    qt_insert_branch
                }
            }

Depends on

Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_insert_node_body := qargs ["node";"d";"x";"y"]
  (Let (Some "tag") (FieldLoad (Var "node") 0)
  (If (qeq (Var "tag") (qi 0))
    (Seq (qt_seed (Var "node") (Var "x") (Var "y")) (qb true))
    (If (qeq (Var "tag") (qi 1))
      (qloads "node" [("px",1%nat);("py",2%nat)]
        (If (qpoint_eq (Var "x") (Var "y") (Var "px") (Var "py")) (qb false) qt_split))
      qt_insert_branch))).

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_insert_node

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert_node

AI-generated explanation

qt_insert_node constructs a call to a recursive expression named "qt_insert_node", passing n, d, x, and y together as its arguments. Its body reads the node’s tag: tag 0 runs qt_seed with the node and coordinates and then returns true; tag 1 compares the coordinates with the stored point, returning false if they match and otherwise running qt_split; any other tag runs qt_insert_branch. The supplied input does not define those helpers or explain d’s role, and it establishes neither correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
qt_insert_node(n, d, x, y)
Rocq structure
definition qt_insert_node:
    parameter(n, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(d, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(x, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(y, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke:
            "qt_insert_node"
            qt_insert_node_body
            list_with_tail_items(n, items(d, x, y))

Prompt 3 · 2026-10-10T13:18:56+00:00
push eerything including fnal report

qt_contains_node_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_contains_node_body

AI-generated explanation

This defines a quadtree lookup body taking a node and coordinates x and y. It reads the node’s tag from field 0: tag 0 returns false, tag 1 compares the query coordinates with the point stored in fields 1 and 2, and any other tag loads split coordinates and four children, chooses a child using strict comparisons with the splits, and calls qt_contains_node on that child. Equality with a split selects the right or upper side. This interpretation assumes suitable node layouts and the intended meanings of helpers whose definitions are not supplied; the declaration itself supplies no correctness contract or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func qt_contains_node(node: QuadNode, x: Int64, y: Int64): Bool {
    let tag = node.tag
    if (tag == 0) {
        false
    } else {
        if (tag == 1) {
            let px = node.px
            let py = node.py
            (x == px) && (y == py)
        } else {
            let mx = node.mx
            let my = node.my
            let ll = node.ll
            let lr = node.lr
            let ul = node.ul
            let ur = node.ur
            let child = if (if (x <= mx) {
                if (x == mx) { false } else { true }
            } else {
                false
            }) {
                if (if (y <= my) {
                    if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) { ll } else { ul }
            } else {
                if (if (y <= my) {
                    if (y == my) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) { lr } else { ur }
            }
            qt_contains_node(child, x, y)
        }
    }
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_contains_node_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list_with_tail_items("node", items("x", "y"))
            let tag = CJR FieldLoad [requires runtime or layout metadata](node, 0)
            if (qeq(tag, qi(0))) {
                qb(false)
            } else {
                if (qeq(tag, qi(1))) {
                    qloads:
                        "node"
                        list_with_tail_items:
                            tuple_with_tail_items("px", items(in_scope nat(1)))
                            items(tuple_with_tail_items("py", items(in_scope nat(2))))
                        qpoint_eq(x, y, px, py)
                } else {
                    qloads:
                        "node"
                        list_with_tail_items:
                            tuple_with_tail_items("mx", items(in_scope nat(7)))
                            items:
                                tuple_with_tail_items("my", items(in_scope nat(8)))
                                tuple_with_tail_items("ll", items(in_scope nat(9)))
                                tuple_with_tail_items("lr", items(in_scope nat(10)))
                                tuple_with_tail_items("ul", items(in_scope nat(11)))
                                tuple_with_tail_items("ur", items(in_scope nat(12)))
                        let child = qchoose(x, y, mx, my, ll, lr, ul, ur)
                        qrecur("qt_contains_node", list_with_tail_items(child, items(x, y)))
                }
            }

Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_contains_node_body := qargs ["node";"x";"y"]
  (Let (Some "tag") (FieldLoad (Var "node") 0)
  (If (qeq (Var "tag") (qi 0)) (qb false)
    (If (qeq (Var "tag") (qi 1))
      (qloads "node" [("px",1%nat);("py",2%nat)]
        (qpoint_eq (Var "x") (Var "y") (Var "px") (Var "py")))
      (qloads "node" [("mx",7%nat);("my",8%nat);("ll",9%nat);("lr",10%nat);("ul",11%nat);("ur",12%nat)]
        (Let (Some "child") (qchoose (Var "x") (Var "y") (Var "mx") (Var "my")
                              (Var "ll") (Var "lr") (Var "ul") (Var "ur"))
          (qrecur "qt_contains_node" [Var "child";Var "x";Var "y"])))))).

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated list concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain D, let Iₚ(S) insert an in-bounds point and Qᵣ(S) select points inside rectangle R.

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** Iₚ(Iᵩ(S)) = Iᵩ(Iₚ(S)), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** Iₚ(Iₚ(S)) = Iₚ(S).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** Qᵣ(S ∪ T) = Qᵣ(S) ∪ Qᵣ(T), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove constructor, insertion, membership, and range-query contracts, then derive operation-composition theorems connecting the concrete programs to the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Coordinates and query endpoints use `Int64`; prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits under the depth restriction. All proofs must close without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and build integration

- Keep example-specific files under `quadtree/`, organized into `spec`, `algorithm`, `proof`, `extracted`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `results`, with local configuration, Makefile, and README.
- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.
- Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.
- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.
- Verification includes `coqchk`, assumption auditing, and checks against admissions or axioms.
- Generate quadtree-specific offline HTML inside `quadtree/docs/`, with links confined to that documentation.
- Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, exposing compiler and SDK paths as runner options.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate full results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. qt_cons

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_cons

AI-generated explanation

qt_cons takes three expression arguments, x, y, and tail, and constructs an application of the named recursive expression "qt_cons" to a structure containing those arguments. Its body uses qargs with the names "x", "y", and "tail", then forms a New expression containing qb true and those three variables. The supplied input does not define qargs, qb, or the runtime meaning of New, so it does not establish the precise allocation behavior or any correctness or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
qt_cons(x, y, tail)
Rocq structure
definition qt_cons:
    parameter(x, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(y, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(tail, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_cons", qt_cons_body, list_with_tail_items(x, items(y, tail)))

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qinvoke
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_cons_body
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_cons x y tail := qinvoke "qt_cons" qt_cons_body [x;y;tail].
