qt_range_branch

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_range_branch

AI-generated explanation

qt_range_branch builds an expression that loads fields 9–12 of node into ll, lr, ul, and ur, then calls qt_range_node on ur, ul, lr, and ll in that order. Each call receives the same a0, b0, a1, and b1 arguments, with acc passed to the first call and each result passed as the accumulator to the next; the last call supplies the overall result. The names suggest quadtree children and range bounds, but their meanings and the required node layout are not established here. This definition provides no correctness or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
let ll = node.f9
let lr = node.f10
let ul = node.f11
let ur = node.f12
let a3 = qt_range_node(ur, a0, b0, a1, b1, acc)
let a2 = qt_range_node(ul, a0, b0, a1, b1, a3)
let a1out = qt_range_node(lr, a0, b0, a1, b1, a2)
qt_range_node(ll, a0, b0, a1, b1, a1out)
Rocq structure
definition qt_range_branch:
    body:
        qloads:
            "node"
            list_with_tail_items:
                tuple_with_tail_items("ll", items(in_scope nat(9)))
                items:
                    tuple_with_tail_items("lr", items(in_scope nat(10)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("ul", items(in_scope nat(11)))
                    tuple_with_tail_items("ur", items(in_scope nat(12)))
            let a3 = 
                qrecur:
                    "qt_range_node"
                    list_with_tail_items(ur, items(a0, b0, a1, b1, acc))
            let a2 = 
                qrecur("qt_range_node", list_with_tail_items(ul, items(a0, b0, a1, b1, a3)))
            let a1out = 
                qrecur("qt_range_node", list_with_tail_items(lr, items(a0, b0, a1, b1, a2)))
            qrecur("qt_range_node", list_with_tail_items(ll, items(a0, b0, a1, b1, a1out)))

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qloads
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qrecur
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_range_branch :=
  qloads "node" [("ll",9%nat);("lr",10%nat);("ul",11%nat);("ur",12%nat)]
    (Let (Some "a3") (qrecur "qt_range_node" [Var "ur";Var "a0";Var "b0";Var "a1";Var "b1";Var "acc"])
    (Let (Some "a2") (qrecur "qt_range_node" [Var "ul";Var "a0";Var "b0";Var "a1";Var "b1";Var "a3"])
    (Let (Some "a1out") (qrecur "qt_range_node" [Var "lr";Var "a0";Var "b0";Var "a1";Var "b1";Var "a2"])
      (qrecur "qt_range_node" [Var "ll";Var "a0";Var "b0";Var "a1";Var "b1";Var "a1out"])))).

- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated list concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain D, let Iₚ(S) insert an in-bounds point and Qᵣ(S) select points inside rectangle R.

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** Iₚ(Iᵩ(S)) = Iᵩ(Iₚ(S)), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** Iₚ(Iₚ(S)) = Iₚ(S).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** Qᵣ(S ∪ T) = Qᵣ(S) ∪ Qᵣ(T), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove constructor, insertion, membership, and range-query contracts, then derive operation-composition theorems connecting the concrete programs to the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Coordinates and query endpoints use `Int64`; prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits under the depth restriction. All proofs must close without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and build integration

- Keep example-specific files under `quadtree/`, organized into `spec`, `algorithm`, `proof`, `extracted`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `results`, with local configuration, Makefile, and README.
- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.
- Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.
- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.
- Verification includes `coqchk`, assumption auditing, and checks against admissions or axioms.
- Generate quadtree-specific offline HTML inside `quadtree/docs/`, with links confined to that documentation.
- Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, exposing compiler and SDK paths as runner options.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate full results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_range_node_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_range_node_body

AI-generated explanation

This defines a quadtree range-query body taking a node, query bounds, and an accumulator. Tag 0 returns the accumulator; otherwise, it reads the node’s bounds and skips nodes that fail the strict overlap test. For an overlapping node with tag 1, it reads a point and calls qt_cons to add it when it lies within the query’s lower-inclusive, upper-exclusive bounds. Other overlapping tags trigger recursive calls on the upper-right, upper-left, lower-right, and lower-left children, passing the accumulator between calls. This interpretation assumes the intended field layout and helper behavior, which are not fully supplied; the definition provides no correctness contract or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func qt_range_node(node: QuadNode, a0: Int64, b0: Int64, a1: Int64, b1: Int64, acc: PointList): PointList {
    let tag = node.tag
    if (tag == 0) {
        acc
    } else {
        let x0 = node.x0
        let y0 = node.y0
        let x1 = node.x1
        let y1 = node.y1
        if ((if (x0 <= a1) {
            if (x0 == a1) { false } else { true }
        } else {
            false
        } && if (a0 <= x1) {
            if (a0 == x1) { false } else { true }
        } else {
            false
        }) && (if (y0 <= b1) {
            if (y0 == b1) { false } else { true }
        } else {
            false
        } && if (b0 <= y1) {
            if (b0 == y1) { false } else { true }
        } else {
            false
        })) {
            if (tag == 1) {
                let px = node.px
                let py = node.py
                if (((a0 <= px) && if (px <= a1) {
                    if (px == a1) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                }) && ((b0 <= py) && if (py <= b1) {
                    if (py == b1) { false } else { true }
                } else {
                    false
                })) {
                    qt_cons(px, py, acc)
                } else {
                    acc
                }
            } else {
                let ll = node.ll
                let lr = node.lr
                let ul = node.ul
                let ur = node.ur
                let a3 = qt_range_node(ur, a0, b0, a1, b1, acc)
                let a2 = qt_range_node(ul, a0, b0, a1, b1, a3)
                let a1out = qt_range_node(lr, a0, b0, a1, b1, a2)
                qt_range_node(ll, a0, b0, a1, b1, a1out)
            }
        } else {
            acc
        }
    }
}

                acc
            } else {
                qloads:
                    "node"

                        items:
                            tuple_with_tail_items("y0", items(in_scope nat(4)))
                            tuple_with_tail_items("x1", items(in_scope nat(5)))
                            tuple_with_tail_items("y1", items(in_scope nat(6)))
                    if (qoverlap(x0, y0, x1, y1, a0, b0, a1, b1)) {
                        if (qeq(tag, qi(1))) {
                            qloads:
                                "node"

                                        tuple_with_tail_items("py", items(in_scope nat(2)))
                                if (qinside(px, py, a0, b0, a1, b1)) {
                                    qt_cons(px, py, acc)
                                } else {
                                    acc
                                }
                        } else {
                            qt_range_branch
                        }

                        acc
                    }

      (Var "acc"))))).

- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

qt_range_node

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_range_node

AI-generated explanation

qt_range_node constructs an embedded program call to qt_range_node_body, named for recursion, with node n, query bounds a, b, c, d, and accumulator acc. The body returns acc for tag 0 or when the node’s bounds fail the overlap test; for tag 1, it adds the stored point to acc if the point passes the inside test, and otherwise returns acc. Other overlapping nodes use qt_range_branch. The supplied input does not define the branch behavior, boundary conventions, or required memory layout, and this definition alone establishes neither correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
qt_range_node(n, a, b, c, d, acc)
Rocq structure
definition qt_range_node:
    parameter(n, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(a, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(b, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(c, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(d, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(acc, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke:
            "qt_range_node"
            qt_range_node_body
            list_with_tail_items(n, items(a, b, c, d, acc))

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. - **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. # Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated list concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain D, let Iₚ(S) insert an in-bounds point and Qᵣ(S) select points inside rectangle R.

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** Iₚ(Iᵩ(S)) = Iᵩ(Iₚ(S)), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** Iₚ(Iₚ(S)) = Iₚ(S).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** Qᵣ(S ∪ T) = Qᵣ(S) ∪ Qᵣ(T), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove constructor, insertion, membership, and range-query contracts, then derive operation-composition theorems connecting the concrete programs to the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Coordinates and query endpoints use `Int64`; prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits under the depth restriction. All proofs must close without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and build integration

- Keep example-specific files under `quadtree/`, organized into `spec`, `algorithm`, `proof`, `extracted`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `results`, with local configuration, Makefile, and README.
- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.
- Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.
- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.
- Verification includes `coqchk`, assumption auditing, and checks against admissions or axioms.
- Generate quadtree-specific offline HTML inside `quadtree/docs/`, with links confined to that documentation.
- Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, exposing compiler and SDK paths as runner options.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate full results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_make_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_make_body

AI-generated explanation

This definition builds a program body with an argument named depth: it calls qt_power on depth and binds the result to side, calls qt_empty with integer zeros and side twice and binds the result to root, then uses New with an empty first list and the values depth, side, and root. The supplied input does not establish what qt_power or qt_empty compute or the precise allocation or layout meaning of New, and it provides no correctness contract, input restrictions, or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func make(depth: Int64): QuadTree {
    let side = qt_power(depth)
    let root = qt_empty(0, 0, side, side)
    QuadTree(depth, side, root)
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_make_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list("depth")
            let side = qt_power(depth)
            let root = qt_empty(qi(0), qi(0), side, side)
            CJR New [requires runtime or layout metadata]:
                empty_list
                list_with_tail_items(depth, items(side, root))

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qargs
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qi
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_empty
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_power
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_make_body := qargs ["depth"]
  (Let (Some "side") (qt_power (Var "depth"))
  (Let (Some "root") (qt_empty (qi 0) (qi 0) (Var "side") (Var "side"))
    (New [] [Var "depth";Var "side";Var "root"]))).

qt_make

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_make

AI-generated explanation

qt_make d constructs a program expression that invokes qt_make_body with the single argument d, using a recursive closure named "qt_make". The body binds an argument named "depth", computes side using qt_power, computes root using qt_empty with two zero expressions and side twice, then uses New with depth, side, and root. The supplied input does not define these helper operations or the runtime layout, so it does not establish the precise resulting structure, correctness, or termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
make(d)
Rocq structure
definition qt_make:
    parameter(d, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_make", qt_make_body, list(d))

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated list concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain D, let Iₚ(S) insert an in-bounds point and Qᵣ(S) select points inside rectangle R.

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** Iₚ(Iᵩ(S)) = Iᵩ(Iₚ(S)), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** Iₚ(Iₚ(S)) = Iₚ(S).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** Qᵣ(S ∪ T) = Qᵣ(S) ∪ Qᵣ(T), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove constructor, insertion, membership, and range-query contracts, then derive operation-composition theorems connecting the concrete programs to the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Coordinates and query endpoints use `Int64`; prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits under the depth restriction. All proofs must close without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and build integration

- Keep example-specific files under `quadtree/`, organized into `spec`, `algorithm`, `proof`, `extracted`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `results`, with local configuration, Makefile, and README.
- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.
- Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.
- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.
- Verification includes `coqchk`, assumption auditing, and checks against admissions or axioms.
- Generate quadtree-specific offline HTML inside `quadtree/docs/`, with links confined to that documentation.
- Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, exposing compiler and SDK paths as runner options.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate full results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_insert_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert_body

AI-generated explanation

This definition builds a program body taking arguments named tree, x, and y. It reads side from tree field 1 and checks whether 0 ≤ x < side and 0 ≤ y < side. If so, it reads depth from field 0 and root from field 2, then invokes qt_insert_node with root, depth, x, and y; otherwise, it returns false. The supplied input does not establish the required tree layout or what the insertion call does, and this definition provides no correctness or termination guarantee.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func insert(tree: QuadTree, x: Int64, y: Int64): Bool {
    let side = tree.side
    if (((0 <= x) && if (x <= side) {
        if (x == side) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) && ((0 <= y) && if (y <= side) {
        if (y == side) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    })) {
        let depth = tree.depth
        let root = tree.root
        qt_insert_node(root, depth, x, y)
    } else {
        false
    }
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_insert_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list_with_tail_items("tree", items("x", "y"))
            qloads:
                "tree"
                list(tuple_with_tail_items("side", items(in_scope nat(1))))
                if (qinside(x, y, qi(0), qi(0), side, side)) {
                    qloads:
                        "tree"
                        list_with_tail_items:
                            tuple_with_tail_items("depth", items(in_scope nat(0)))
                            items(tuple_with_tail_items("root", items(in_scope nat(2))))
                        qt_insert_node(root, depth, x, y)
                } else {
                    qb(false)
                }

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qargs
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qb
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qi
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qinside
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qloads
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert_node
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_insert_body := qargs ["tree";"x";"y"]
  (qloads "tree" [("side",1%nat)]
  (If (qinside (Var "x") (Var "y") (qi 0) (qi 0) (Var "side") (Var "side"))
    (qloads "tree" [("depth",0%nat);("root",2%nat)]
      (qt_insert_node (Var "root") (Var "depth") (Var "x") (Var "y"))) (qb false))).

## Summary

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.

- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

qt_insert

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_insert

AI-generated explanation

qt_insert t x y builds an invocation named "qt_insert" with arguments t, x, and y. Its body binds these as tree, x, and y, loads side from tree field 1, and tests qinside using the coordinates and bounds (0, 0, side, side). If the test succeeds, it loads depth from field 0 and root from field 2 and calls qt_insert_node; otherwise, it returns false. The supplied input does not establish the precise boundary test, memory requirements, insertion behavior, or termination, and provides no correctness contract.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
insert(t, x, y)
Rocq structure
definition qt_insert:
    parameter(t, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(x, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(y, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_insert", qt_insert_body, list_with_tail_items(t, items(x, y)))

- Generate Cangjie directly from verified CJR definitions using a local extraction entry point and the shared Gallina printer. Do not hand-edit generated implementation code.

- Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Test empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees for S, T, and S ∪ T, then check range distribution using deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain range queries.

qt_contains_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_contains_body

AI-generated explanation

This definition builds a program body with arguments tree, x, and y. It loads side from field 1 of tree and checks whether 0 ≤ x < side and 0 ≤ y < side. If so, it loads root from field 2 and calls qt_contains_node(root, x, y); otherwise, it returns false. The supplied input does not show the node-search implementation or establish that the field accesses are valid, the search correctly tests membership, or execution terminates; this is a program definition, not a correctness contract.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func contains(tree: QuadTree, x: Int64, y: Int64): Bool {
    let side = tree.side
    if (((0 <= x) && if (x <= side) {
        if (x == side) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    }) && ((0 <= y) && if (y <= side) {
        if (y == side) { false } else { true }
    } else {
        false
    })) {
        let root = tree.root
        qt_contains_node(root, x, y)
    } else {
        false
    }
}
Rocq structure
definition qt_contains_body:
    body:
        qargs:
            list_with_tail_items("tree", items("x", "y"))
            qloads:
                "tree"
                list(tuple_with_tail_items("side", items(in_scope nat(1))))
                if (qinside(x, y, qi(0), qi(0), side, side)) {
                    qloads:
                        "tree"
                        list(tuple_with_tail_items("root", items(in_scope nat(2))))
                        qt_contains_node(root, x, y)
                } else {
                    qb(false)
                }

Depends on

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qargs

  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qinside
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qloads
  • cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_contains_node
Original declaration (proof-free)
Definition qt_contains_body := qargs ["tree";"x";"y"]
  (qloads "tree" [("side",1%nat)]
  (If (qinside (Var "x") (Var "y") (qi 0) (qi 0) (Var "side") (Var "side"))
    (qloads "tree" [("root",2%nat)]
      (qt_contains_node (Var "root") (Var "x") (Var "y"))) (qb false))).

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_contains

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_contains

AI-generated explanation

qt_contains builds an invocation of the expression qt_contains_body with arguments t, x, and y, using a recursive closure named "qt_contains". The body binds these arguments as tree, x, and y, loads side from tree field 1, and tests whether the point lies inside the region described by (0, 0, side, side). If so, it loads root from field 2 and calls qt_contains_node; otherwise it returns false. The supplied input does not define the precise boundary test or node-search behavior, and this definition alone establishes neither correctness nor termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
contains(t, x, y)
Rocq structure
definition qt_contains:
    parameter(t, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(x, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(y, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_contains", qt_contains_body, list_with_tail_items(t, items(x, y)))

qt_range_body

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_range_body

AI-generated explanation

qt_range_body defines a program body for arguments named tree, a0, b0, a1, and b1: it loads field 2 of tree into root, binds acc to the result of qt_nil, then invokes qt_range_node with root, the four coordinate-like arguments, and acc. The names suggest a quadtree range query with an initially empty accumulator, but the supplied definitions do not establish the bounds’ meaning, qt_nil’s result, or the query’s behavior. No correctness contract or termination guarantee is provided.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
func range(tree: QuadTree, a0: Int64, b0: Int64, a1: Int64, b1: Int64): PointList {
    let root = tree.root
    let acc = qt_nil()
    qt_range_node(root, a0, b0, a1, b1, acc)
}

# Verified quadtree example with an HTML report index

## Summary

Create a self-contained integer-point quadtree example under `quadtree/`, reusing the shared CJR/Iris framework and Gallina printer.

The final deliverable is **`quadtree/index.html`**: an offline HTML index linking individual rendered definitions and lemmas, extracted Cangjie files, the test report, and the benchmark report.

## Interface and algorithm

- `make(depth)` creates an empty tree covering `[0, 2^depth) × [0, 2^depth)`, requiring `0 ≤ depth ≤ 30`.
- `insert(tree, x, y)` returns `true` exactly when it adds a new in-bounds point. Duplicate or out-of-bounds insertion returns `false` and preserves the tree.
- `contains(tree, x, y)` returns exact membership; out-of-bounds coordinates return `false`.
- `range(tree, xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)` returns a duplicate-free linked list of matching points. Rectangles are half-open; empty or reversed rectangles produce empty results. Result order is unspecified.
- Nodes are empty leaves, single-point leaves, or four-child branches. Quadrants are lower-left, lower-right, upper-left, upper-right; midpoint coordinates belong to upper/right halves.
- Splitting redistributes the existing point before inserting the new point. Distinct integer points separate before reaching unit cells.
- Store node bounds and branch midpoints. Calculate subdivision sizes using a verified doubling helper and existing CJR arithmetic.
- Range traversal prunes disjoint nodes and accumulates results without repeated concatenation.

## Specification and verification

Define a finite-set specification and executable Gallina reference model. For fixed domain \(D\), let \(I_p(S)\) insert an in-bounds point and \(Q_R(S)\) select points inside rectangle \(R\).

Prove:

- **Insertion commutativity:** \(I_p(I_q(S)) = I_q(I_p(S))\), including duplicate and rejected coordinates.
- **Insertion idempotence:** \(I_p(I_p(S)) = I_p(S)\).
- **Repeated-query idempotence:** repeated membership calls return the same Boolean; repeated range calls return equivalent duplicate-free lists while preserving the tree’s fields and structure.
- **Range distribution over union:** \(Q_R(S \cup T) = Q_R(S) \cup Q_R(T)\), for trees sharing a domain. Union is specification-level set union, with overlapping points included once.
- Subdivision coverage/disjointness, bounded depth, uniqueness, and exact operation semantics.

Equality in insertion laws concerns represented point sets. Query results are compared as sets or permutations; fresh allocations are allowed.

Define Iris ownership predicates for trees and result lists. Prove operation contracts and derive concrete operation-composition theorems validating the algebraic laws. Query contracts preserve the same structural tree model.

Use `Int64` coordinates and query endpoints, and prove stored-bound and midpoint calculations stay within its limits. Verification must pass `coqchk` and assumption auditing without admissions or new axioms.

## Directory layout and final HTML report

Keep example-specific specification, algorithm, proofs, extraction entry point, generated code, tests, benchmarks, results, and documentation under `quadtree/`. Provide local `build`, `verify`, `extract`, `test`, `benchmark`, `docs`, and `report` targets.

Root changes are limited to load paths, build/verification wiring, a README pointer, and required repository logs.

The report index contains:

- **Definitions and lemmas:** grouped by specification/model, algorithm, ownership predicates, correctness, and algebraic laws. Each entry links directly to its declaration anchor in a page generated by `rocq_readable`, with proofs omitted. Build this index from the renderer’s generated declaration navigation and stable anchors.
- **Extracted Cangjie:** list every generated `.cj` file, linking both the original file and an escaped HTML source view. Extract implementation code directly from verified CJR definitions.
- **Test report:** link an HTML report containing case results, randomized/algebraic validation summaries, compiler information, source hashes, and execution status.
- **Benchmark report:** link an HTML report containing workload descriptions, timing summaries, local charts, comparison methodology, machine/compiler metadata, and downloadable raw CSV/JSON results.
- **Verification summary:** show proof-checking and assumption-audit results, along with renderer errors or unsupported-syntax coverage.

Use relative links, local CSS and chart assets, escaped content, and no network dependencies. All report destinations remain inside `quadtree/`.

`make report` assembles the index after verification, extraction, documentation, tests, and benchmarks. Assembly fails if required artifacts are missing or recorded source hashes are stale. Keep generated HTML, extracted code, and report data in version control.

## Tests, benchmarks, and acceptance

Test empty trees, depth zero, domain boundaries, midpoint routing, duplicates, rejected coordinates, all quadrants, and deeply clustered points. Cover empty, reversed, disjoint, selective, overlapping, and whole-domain rectangles.

Validate algebraic laws on the unchanged extracted code:

- Insert point pairs in both orders and compare complete contents and membership.
- Repeat queries and check equivalent results and unchanged tree contents.
- Build trees representing \(S\), \(T\), and \(S \cup T\), then check range distribution with deduplicated result sets.
- Run seeded randomized operation sequences and insertion-order permutations against an independent flat-set oracle.

Compile and run using Cangjie 1.0.5, with configurable compiler and SDK paths.

Benchmark extracted CJR against an independent native Cangjie quadtree with identical semantics and subdivision policy, plus a flat collection using hash-indexed membership and range scanning. Use uniform, clustered, and deeply coincident-prefix datasets at 256, 4,096, and 16,384 unique points. Measure construction/insertion, membership hits/misses, and empty, selective, broad, and whole-domain queries.

Use two warmups, calibration toward 20 ms batches, seven trials across three processes, rotating implementation order, and allocation-aware repetition limits. Validate complete results outside timing and consume checked checksums inside measured batches. Save raw observations, medians/IQR, source hashes, compiler flags, machine metadata, and charts.

Acceptance requires completed functional and algebraic proofs, reproducible extraction, passing runtime tests, measured benchmarks, and a complete HTML index whose file links and declaration anchors all resolve. Visually inspect the index and representative linked pages at desktop and narrow widths.

Initial scope defers deletion, root expansion, and an executable union API.

qt_range

cjr.quadtree.algorithm.program.qt_range

AI-generated explanation

qt_range constructs an expression that invokes qt_range_body under the name "qt_range", passing t, a, b, c, and d as arguments packaged in a structure. The body names these arguments tree, a0, b0, a1, and b1, loads root from tree using index 2, initializes acc to qt_nil, and calls qt_range_node with the root, four supplied values, and accumulator. The names suggest a quadtree range query, but the supplied definitions do not establish its precise behavior, required input conditions, correctness, or termination.

Cangjie · Gallina printer
range(t, a, b, c, d)
Rocq structure
definition qt_range:
    parameter(t, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(a, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(b, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(c, type(_ (inferred)))
    parameter(d, type(_ (inferred)))
    body:
        qinvoke("qt_range", qt_range_body, list_with_tail_items(t, items(a, b, c, d)))
